### 2. lookup_tyre_pressure — PDF 90/18, printed 88/16

Specify 32 psi (2.24 kg/cm²), front and rear, solo or pillion. Preserve the underinflation overheating/damage/injury warning. 'Royal Enfield specified tyres' is too vague if the rider needs a replacement: supply the exact front CEAT Gripp RE F, 90/90-21 M/C 54H and rear CEAT Gripp Rad steel RE, 140/80R17 M/C 69H from page 18.

Ask whether fitted tyres are tube-type, tubeless or unknown; distinguish this from rim type. Show the exact front/rear models in both branches, together with the applicable rim requirement. For unknown construction show both rim-dependent requirements and the support route. The table does not label separate tube/tubeless model versions: do not invent that distinction or infer compatibility of another brand. Never infer adequate fitment from dimensions alone. These extra questions belong to replacement selection, not the basic pressure-only lookup.

Rider: What tyre pressure should I use?

## Owner feedback 3: tube/tubeless question, 9 October 2026

Owner requests asking what is fitted to the vehicle, tube or tubeless, answering based on that input, and showing both options when the rider does not know. Recorded as requested dialogue change; revised exact wording remains pending.

Source boundary: PDF page 18 names front CEAT Gripp RE F 90/90-21 M/C 54H and rear CEAT Gripp Rad steel RE 140/80R17 M/C 69H, and gives separate tube-type/tubeless-type rim requirements. That table does not identify a separate named model list for each construction. Do not manufacture that mapping or infer rim type from the rider's description of a currently fitted tyre.

Proposed follow-up:
Assistant: Are the tyres currently fitted to your bike tube-type, tubeless, or are you unsure?
If tube-type: The manual says a tube-type rim requires a tyre fitted with a tube. Before recommending a replacement, is your rim also tube-type?
If tubeless: The manual says a tubeless-type rim must use only a tubeless-type tyre. Before recommending a replacement, is your rim also tubeless-type?
If unsure: The manual gives two rim-dependent options: on a tube-type rim, fit the tyre with a tube; on a tubeless-type rim, use only a tubeless-type tyre. It lists the front and rear CEAT specifications above but does not name separate tube and tubeless models. Kindly write to Royal Enfield at support@royalenfield.com to confirm the correct version for your wheels.

For a confirmed matching rim: show the applicable rim requirement with the manual's front/rear specifications, without asserting that the named models establish a specific construction or approving a purchasable replacement version. For unknown/mismatched rim or alternative brand/version: withhold replacement compatibility recommendation and use verified support route. Rider input is recorded as a reported observation, not independently verified fitment. Pressure lookup remains a separate direct answer and does not need this construction question because the manual lists the same 32 psi value for front/rear and solo/pillion without a construction split.

No blanket or formal source approval, code change, deployment or message to another chat follows from this feedback.

Identify F3, red, 10 A mini-blade. ## Card 3: lookup_fan_fuse

Status: PRESENTED FOR REVIEW; no owner response yet.

Original PDF page 112, printed page 110, Minor maintenance tips / Mini blade fuse usage list. Agent independently inspected original full-page rendering and text. F3 row: Red, 10 A, Fan. Exact selected Himalayan BS6 manual applies.

Proposed conversation:
Rider: Which fuse protects the fan, and what rating is it?
Assistant: The fan uses fuse F3, a red 10 A mini-blade fuse.
Conditional safety wording: If replacing a failed fuse, switch the ignition OFF and use a new fuse of the same rating. Never bridge/jumper it or use other conductive material; this risks short-circuit and fire. Wrong-rating or low-grade fuses can damage the electrical system. After any fuse failure, have the electrical system thoroughly checked and faults corrected immediately to avoid repeated failure. If you use a spare, replenish the spare fuse in its holder at the earliest opportunity.

Stopping scope: direct identification/rating lookup; no claim the fuse is blown or caused a fan symptom, no access/removal procedure, no automatic replacement recommendation. Warning and ignition condition included conditionally because current draft mentions replacement and requires these dependencies. Physical access and unresolved-action stopping rule will be separately reviewed with a_fuse. Owner checks requested: exact F3/red/10 A/Fan row, all relevant warning paragraphs, ignition OFF note, and whether wording clearly separates lookup from conditional replacement warnings. Complete conditional wording is proposed engineering review content and remains unapproved.

Current feedback-guide consolidation (engineering context):

### 4. lookup_starter_duration — PDF 72–74/121, printed 70–72/119

Lead with 'until it starts, maximum 5 seconds' conditional on one compact checklist: neutral and neutral lamp, ignition ON, right-hand kill switch RUN, adequate fuel/no continuously blinking final bar, clutch pulled in and held, side stand retracted. If no start within five seconds, release and wait about five seconds; preserve the prolonged-holding/battery-below-10-V caution. Do not turn the maximum into an instruction to hold five seconds after start.

Avoid seven separate setup turns. Branch on the item the rider flags, retain confirmed answers, and record unsuccessful attempts separately. Low fuel leads to the separately reviewed refuelling action with warnings. For continued no-start, ask the documented dim-lights/weak-horn observation and select only reviewed applicable checks. A negative electrical clue does not prove battery health. MIL/EFI pattern is described after engine start; do not use that pattern to clear EFI while no start persists. Starter-sound descriptions may clarify what the rider means but have no source-backed diagnostic rule in these pages. Do not invent one. The manual's multiple-attempt ignition-cycle note has no numerical retry count; do not invent an unlimited retry policy.


## New starting-source addition pending acceptance

Original PDF75/printed73, inspected after records4/7 acceptance, adds15–20second battery recovery after three successive cranking attempts. Propose this alongside max5second attempts and page74 about5second interval, retaining no automatic repeats. It also supplies post-start idle/acceleration cautions that require scoped review when actual restart/riding guidance is included. Complete procedure boundaries can extend beyond the pages originally proposed; actively check continuation pages and invalidate incomplete dependency assumptions. Owner acceptance of earlier wording does not waive newly found mandatory source context.

## Card 4: lookup_starter_duration

Status: PRESENTED FOR REVIEW; no owner response yet. Original PDF pages 72/73/74, printed pages 70/71/72, Starting. Agent visually inspected all three complete pages independently of retrieval.

Proposed direct Q&A: Rider asks how long to hold starter. Assistant says maximum 5 seconds per attempt, release when engine starts; if no start within 5 seconds, release and wait about 5 seconds before another attempt. Do not keep holding for long periods; manual warns battery can drain below 10 V and directs authorised-service inspection to identify/correct no-start cause. This warning is not a diagnostic assertion that the rider battery is below 10 V.

Before an actual starting attempt: neutral and neutral indicator glowing (72); ignition ON/kill switch RUN (72); adequate fuel, last fuel bar continuously blinking means refuel immediately (73); hold clutch lever in (73); side stand retracted before starting/moving (74). If malfunction indicator does not turn OFF as described after starting, authorised-service inspection (72-73). Lookup is not a complete guided starting procedure or no-start diagnosis.

Potential continuation: page 74 says ignition OFF then ON and start again after multiple continuous unsuccessful attempts, but gives no numerical attempt limit. Show this note as a scope question for human review; do not invent a retry count or present unlimited retry permission. Proposed unresolved response refers rider to authorised service and does not automatically loop. Fixed report is customer-reported resolution, not verified fault diagnosis.

Owner checks requested: maximum 5 seconds on page 73; about 5-second wait and full battery/service caution on page 74; prerequisite scope on 72-74; treatment of multiple-attempt ignition-cycle note versus service stopping rule. All underlying approvals remain pending.



## Owner feedback 9: starter eligibility and no-start branches, 9 October 2026

Owner requires checking gear neutral, neutral lamp, ignition ON and right-handlebar kill switch RUN before starter advice. Requests consideration of EFI/MIL, starter sound, low fuel/final blinking bar, and continued diagnostic questions after tried-but-no-start. This rejects the immediate starter-instruction/service-only draft; revised exact dialogue remains pending.

Revised proposal: after confirmed Himalayan BS6/manual selection, ask one question per turn, reusing confirmed facts: gear neutral (72), neutral lamp glowing (72), ignition key ON (72), right-handlebar kill switch RUN (72), final fuel bar continuously blinking (73; yes blocks starting and routes to separately reviewed refuelling action), adequate fuel if not established (73; no routes refuelling; absence of blinking does not prove adequate fuel), clutch held in (73), side stand retracted (74). Only once starting conditions are established, give max-5-second instruction and about-5-second interval, with full battery drain/service caution. Do not invent an unsourced way to verify an unknown fact; withhold start action and clarify or refer. Negative switch/stand observations lead only to separately reviewed corresponding action, never inferred diagnosis. All sequence ordering is product dialogue design, not OEM diagnostic order.

After tried-but-no-start: record ACTION_COMPLETED_UNRESOLVED, preserve confirmed setup facts, clarify any genuinely unknown prerequisite, and ask lights dim or horn weak (121) as an independently documented electrical clue. Yes routes authorised service without diagnosing a specific electrical fault; no does not establish battery/charging health. Other remaining documented no-start checks require their own reviewed source/rule gates, with mandatory fuse inspection/referral policy and stopping conditions preserved. Do not instruct repeated starting or automatically re-run confirmed checks.

EFI source limit: pages 72-73 describe MIL briefly glowing then turning OFF after engine starts; if it does not turn OFF as described, service rectification. Ask about this pattern after reported start, not as a rule eliminating EFI faults while engine still will not start. Normal MIL pattern is only the manual-described indicator behaviour, not verified health of all EFI components. Customer-reported start remains customer-reported resolution; indicator issue may separately require service.

Starter sound: page 121 table has weak horn/dim lights clue, not a starter-sound diagnostic criterion. May collect voluntary sound description for service context if owner wants it; do not infer battery/starter faults or select repairs from that sound. It is not a source-derived branch for this record. Exact revised card and added page-121 dependency require review; no source/generator edits or formal approvals.



## Owner feedback 10: compact conditional starter answer, 9 October 2026

Owner rejects many sequential setup turns. Requests leading with five-second starter answer conditioned on one combined setup checklist, followed by branches only for conditions the rider flags or an unclear result such as no sound. This explicit preference supersedes the previous one-question-per-turn interpretation for presentation of the setup checklist. Keep focused follow-ups where needed.

Revised exact proposal: Hold the starter until the engine starts, for a maximum of 5 seconds, provided the following setup is satisfied: gear neutral and neutral lamp glowing; ignition key ON; right-hand handlebar engine kill switch RUN; adequate fuel and final fuel-gauge bar not blinking continuously; clutch lever pulled in and held; side stand fully retracted. If no start within 5 seconds, release and wait about 5 seconds before another attempt. Do not keep holding: manual warns battery can drain below 10 V. If any condition is not met or rider is unsure, ask them to name that condition and withhold the starting attempt until clarified/corrected through reviewed guidance. A nonblinking fuel bar alone does not establish adequate fuel. Five seconds is a maximum, not a required duration after the engine starts. Conditional text does not assert rider actually satisfied prerequisites or authorize runtime action before eligibility facts are established.

Branches: neutral/neutral-lamp uncertainty clarifies that observation; ignition OFF or kill switch not RUN routes reviewed switches guidance; inadequate fuel or blinking last bar routes reviewed refuelling with complete warnings; clutch not held blocks attempt until held; stand down routes reviewed stand guidance; tried-but-no-start stores unresolved attempt and asks documented dim-lights/weak-horn clue if not already known, then other independently documented eligible checks or authorised service. No automatic repeats or invented OEM diagnostic ordering. No-sound statement asks which sound rider means (starter versus horn), without diagnosing from absent starter sound. MIL question applies only after engine starts, and sustained indicator as described requires authorised service. Revised compact card remains pending scoped acceptance. Source/generator files and formal review ledger untouched.

### 5. lookup_usb_charging — PDF 65, printed 63

For 'why no charging with engine off', answer only that the charger works with engine running, plus relevant cap closure/locking and source warranty caution. Remove unrelated data-transfer, fast/dash-charging and charging-time information. For running-engine failure ask about a standard approved high-quality cable; if cable is fine, ask whether the cap is damaged or fails to close/seal. ## Card 5: lookup_usb_charging

Status: PRESENTED FOR REVIEW; no owner response yet. PDF page 65, printed page 63, Operation of controls. Original complete page visually inspected and text read by agent independently of retrieval.

Proposed Q&A: Rider asks why USB port does not charge when engine is off. Assistant says USB charger operates only while engine is running; port is charging-only, no data transfer or fast/dash charging. Charging time varies by phone capacity, charge level and circuitry, and may be longer than the original phone charger.

Conditions/cautions: use standard approved high-quality USB cables; check phone current requirements before use; manual says charger shuts down if draw exceeds 2.5 A (not a claim that 2.5 A is nominal output or diagnosis of rider failure). Close and lock cap when not in use; manual says warranty will not be provided if cap is damaged and port not properly sealed. Do not infer broader warranty scope.

Proposed follow-up if engine runs but charging still fails: ask whether cable is standard approved/high quality and phone's current requirements are known, rather than diagnose charging-port fault. Unknown current is unknown, not confirmed excessive draw. Source gives no fault-repair sequence or automatic reset instruction. If issue remains unexplained, state cannot verify a further remedy from the manual and use support@royalenfield.com route. No instruction to run engine solely to test charger.

Original draft action-evidence IDs cover engine-running statement only although claim also includes data/fast-charging facts: source author must separately map those claims to the relevant already-required context objects. No source/generator edits or formal approvals. Owner checks requested: engine-running condition; charging-only/no data/no fast charging notes; exact >2.5 A shutdown threshold; complete cable/cap/warranty cautions; lookup stopping scope and unsupported follow-up.



## Owner feedback 13: USB answer relevance and cap branch, 9 October 2026

Owner removes unrelated charging-only/data-transfer, fast/dash charging and charging-time information from the engine-off question. Accepts cap closure/locking caution and warranty wording; engine-running failure may ask about cable and cap damage. Accepts shutdown threshold as a warning only, not a measurable rider fact or observed cause. Supports escalation when no further repair is documented. Revised exact card remains pending; feedback is not acceptance of all original card wording.

Revised first Q&A:
Rider: Why does the USB port not charge when the engine is off?
Assistant: The USB charger works only while the engine is running. Keep the port cap closed and locked when not in use. The manual warns that warranty will not be provided if the cap is damaged and the port is not properly sealed.

Revised follow-up:
Rider: The engine is running, but it still will not charge.
Assistant: Are you using a standard approved, high-quality USB cable?
Rider: Yes, the cable is fine.
Assistant: Is the USB port cap damaged, or does it fail to close and seal properly?
If damaged/unsealed: report the observed cap condition to an authorised Royal Enfield service centre; do not infer that it caused charging failure or decide warranty eligibility.
If intact or unknown and charging remains unresolved: cannot verify a further repair procedure from this manual; kindly write to Royal Enfield at support@royalenfield.com.

## Accepted record 11: an intuitive cooling criterion still needs source support

Record a_cool_engine, original PDF122/121 (printed120/119), with temperature-display context37/38 (printed35/36). Owner accepted outcome branches and proposed room-temperature/overnight-like cooling. The supporting original row only says switchOFF and allowcool; no supported cooling duration, overheating threshold or restart-clearance temperature established.

Owner closed the record with source-supported wording; the proposed room-temperature/overnight endpoint is excluded. Do not turn it into an OEM instruction or claim that ambient cooling verifies safe operation. Accept outcome branches separately from unsupported additions. Retain the source-backed high-level cooling action and state the specific missing temperature/time guidance, with service referral if uncertainty remains. Numerical display ranges and examples do not authorise a hot/not-hot diagnosis. Alternative fuel adulteration row does not license inferring contamination when cooling fails. Customer-reported improvement does not verify overheating cause. Accepted wording: Switch the engineOFF and allow it to cool. The manual does not specify a cooling time or temperature at which restarting is safe. If unsure, have an authorised Royal Enfield service centre assess it.

# EXP001 v3 - Hazards cannot start with ignition OFF

Generated based on feedback from the first 11 cases.

Status: DRAFT; tuning/regression candidate only. No human source approval or runtime authorization.
Manual: Royal Enfield Himalayan BS6 owner's manual, RAM00818/A, 4 November 2025.
SHA-256: cf221a29e2f9cd5af6e80a99774a697c814e8eda661c27d72e519d8a7e80da89.
Type: control clarification. Applicability: confirmed Himalayan + BS6 selection; unresolved actual variant/display differences block specific guidance.

Original pages: PDF 29-30; printed 27-28. Author read original PDF text, not retrieval output. Independent full-page visual/source review remains required, including any figure and continuation boundary.

Proposed engineering conversation:

Rider: "My hazard lights will not turn on with the key OFF."

Assistant: "The manual says hazards cannot be activated with ignition OFF. The hazard button is the triangle-marked button on the left-hand handlebar control shown on PDF29. Can you match that button on your bike? If yes, activate hazards with ignition ON. If you then turn the ignition OFF, they continue for up to 30 minutes unless manually switched off. Hazard flashing strains the battery; avoid prolonged use unless necessary. Turn signals do not work while hazards are ON. For prolonged stops turn the ignition key OFF to avoid battery discharge."

Follow-up/outcome handling: If already activated but not operating as described, do not infer a relay or fuse fault: the cited pages give no further repair process; authorised service. If unsure whether previously activated, clarify that observation. If control differs, reconfirm selected manual.

Source checks for reviewer: Check all three activation states and the continuation-page turn-signal warning; do not imply hazards can always start key-OFF.

Scope and stop: this card supplies only the cited answer/action, not an OEM diagnostic order. Uncertain rider facts remain UNKNOWN. Attempted-but-unresolved actions remain ACTION_COMPLETED_UNRESOLVED, never normal. Success is customer-reported resolution only; preserve separate open issues. No repeat without valid correction/new applicability or an explicit manual requirement. Source gaps explain missing guidance; operational errors return SYSTEM_ERROR, not claims of absent manual information. Service wording: "Kindly write to Royal Enfield at support@royalenfield.com" if service location/support is needed.

Display: all references/excerpts/figures are engineering review context; no public excerpt/crop permission is implied. Runtime answers require approved immutable evidence IDs and dependencies, not these draft page references.

Revision provenance: v2 responds to Agent2 and Agent3 feedback on immutable v1; practical identification, relevant content and complete navigation context. Previous version retained. Exact new content requires re-review; not human approval.

Revision provenance: owner-requested origin declaration added; previous frozen content preserved. This provenance does not imply approval.

# EXP011 v2 - ABS light while stationary

Generated based on feedback from the first 11 cases.

Manual: Royal Enfield Himalayan BS6 owner's manual, RAM00818/A,4 November2025.
SHA-256: cf221a29e2f9cd5af6e80a99774a697c814e8eda661c27d72e519d8a7e80da89.
Type: ambiguity. Risk: high.
Original source: PDF 63,66-69; printed 61,64-67. Original page text and author-rendered complete-page contact sheets inspected where available. This is author inspection only; reviewer must independently inspect exact full pages, continuations, tables, figures, and required warning scope. PDF103 intentionally remains an extraction/source-review gap. No retrieval result used as proof.

Proposed engineering conversation:

Rider: "My ABS lamp is ON."

Assistant: "Is it only ON while stationary/below 5 km/h, or did it remain continuously ON above 5 km/h? The manual describes ignition/kill-switch ON illumination until 5 km/h. Do not ride solely to test it. If continuously ON above 5 km/h, do not continue riding; have authorised service inspect and correct the brake system. Failure can cause serious injury or loss of life."

Follow-up conversations/outcomes: Unknown speed/history: clarify an existing observation without ordering a road test. At rest alone: no fault diagnosis; persistent/malfunction concern service. Above-threshold known stop takes precedence over other failures.

Exact review checks and source limits: Preserve startup state versus persistent malfunction and no induced speed test.

Scope: DRAFT tuning/regression only; no human approval, release, cached answer or runtime authority. Confirm Himalayan+BS6 selection and exact manual/checksum first. Actual controls/variant mismatch blocks action. Direct facts need approved complete evidence just as actions do. Page references are author provenance, not immutable runtime evidence IDs. Source excerpts and figures remain engineering-only; no new public PDF/crop permission.

State and stops (PRODUCT_POLICY): keep missing/unclear facts UNKNOWN; normal observations never prove hidden system health. Preserve ACTION_COMPLETED_UNRESOLVED separately, avoid unjustified repeats, and retain separate open issues after customer-reported resolution. Never claim field repair verified. Table order is not OEM diagnostic order. Missing source dependencies block generation and cannot be solved by rider questions. Distinguish SUPPORTED/AMBIGUOUS/UNSUPPORTED/SAFETY_STOP/SYSTEM_ERROR under TRDv0.4. Explicit OEM service stops dominate additional checks. Support wording: Kindly write to Royal Enfield at support@royalenfield.com.

Generated based on feedback from the first 11 cases.

Manual: Royal Enfield Himalayan BS6 owner's manual, RAM00818/A,4 November2025.

Rider: "Should I release the brake when it pulses during emergency braking?"

Assistant: "The manual says not to release the brake lever/pedal when pulsations occur during hard braking in an emergency; these indicate ABS activation. Apply both brakes simultaneously. ABS does not substitute for careful riding or anticipation and does not reduce braking distance; in some circumstances it can lengthen it. Ride and brake carefully, especially while cornering. Anticipate the stopping distance for your speed and brake well in advance. The instrument cluster must function properly as it is integral to ABS. Failing to follow these cautions can cause an accident with serious injury or loss of life."

Manual: Royal Enfield Himalayan BS6 owner's manual, RAM00818/A,4 November2025.

Rider: "The bike fell on its side with the engine running in gear and then stopped."

Assistant: "The manual describes roll-over-sensor shutdown in that situation. Before its reset, the bike must be safely upright on its centre stand on firm flat ground, in neutral with the neutral lamp glowing. Take extreme care: a fall can injure you or others and damage the bike. Only do this once the motorcycle is already securely supported. The reset guidance does not provide instructions for lifting a fallen bike or safely placing it on the stand. If you can safely confirm those conditions, switch both ignition and stop switches OFF, wait a few seconds, then switch them ON. This does not establish that the bike is undamaged or safe to ride; if condition after the fall is uncertain, have authorised service inspect it."

# EXP038 v3 - Headlamp vertical adjustment scope

Generated based on feedback from the first 11 cases.

Manual: Royal Enfield Himalayan BS6 owner's manual, RAM00818/A,4 November2025.
SHA-256: cf221a29e2f9cd5af6e80a99774a697c814e8eda661c27d72e519d8a7e80da89.
Type: source-limited action. Risk: medium.
Original source: PDF 109; printed 107. Original page text and author-rendered complete-page contact sheets inspected where available. This is author inspection only; reviewer must independently inspect exact full pages, continuations, tables, figures, and required warning scope. PDF103 intentionally remains an extraction/source-review gap. No retrieval result used as proof.

Proposed engineering conversation:

Rider: "Which direction can the headlamp beam be adjusted?"

Assistant: "The manual describes vertical adjustment using the adjusting screw turned clockwise or anticlockwise. It does not map each direction to up/down or supply an aiming distance/height in this guidance, so I cannot prescribe a precise aim setting. Authorised service can set it."

Follow-up conversations/outcomes: No invented wall distance/height; Rider: "I cannot identify the screw." Assistant: "The PDF109 drawing arrow indicates the adjusting screw on the bracket at the rear/lower part of the headlamp. Can you match that screw on your bike?" The figure is engineering review context and public display needs scoped permission. If unable to identify it, or exact aiming requested, explain the missing direction/aim geometry and refer authorised service; do not remove the screw or guide alignment. Do not claim road legality or alignment from photo.

Exact review checks and source limits: Direct movement-scope answer only, no guessed clockwise=up.

Scope: DRAFT tuning/regression only; no human approval, release, cached answer or runtime authority. Confirm Himalayan+BS6 selection and exact manual/checksum first. Actual controls/variant mismatch blocks action. Direct facts need approved complete evidence just as actions do. Page references are author provenance, not immutable runtime evidence IDs. Source excerpts and figures remain engineering-only; no new public PDF/crop permission.

State and stops (PRODUCT_POLICY): keep missing/unclear facts UNKNOWN; normal observations never prove hidden system health. Preserve ACTION_COMPLETED_UNRESOLVED separately, avoid unjustified repeats, and retain separate open issues after customer-reported resolution. Never claim field repair verified. Table order is not OEM diagnostic order. Missing source dependencies block generation and cannot be solved by rider questions. Distinguish SUPPORTED/AMBIGUOUS/UNSUPPORTED/SAFETY_STOP/SYSTEM_ERROR under TRDv0.4. Explicit OEM service stops dominate additional checks. Support wording: Kindly write to Royal Enfield at support@royalenfield.com.

Revision provenance: owner-requested origin declaration added; previous frozen content preserved. This provenance does not imply approval.
